Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING WHEN APPLICABLE**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code, except where the decoder input and output specifications or training a neural decoder require otherwise. You will be assessed on the statistics and decoder accuracy of your converted data, and on whether an expert would consider each of your decisions well justified.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository when applicable. This requires examining the provided paper, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `/app/paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `/app/code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `/app/data`.\n- **Documentation** on nwb file format is at https://nwb-schema.readthedocs.io/en/latest/\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on start of the trial. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n  - 3: 0 cm\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n  - 6: > +50 cm\n- Absolute position in corridor, time-varying. Discretized into 5 equal-sized bins spanning the 450 cm track:\n  - 0: < 90 cm\n  - 1: 90 to 180 cm\n  - 2: 180 to 270 cm\n  - 3: 270 to 360 cm\n  - 4: > 360 cm\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder.\n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. Be critical of results **after every step of processing**. \n\nYou will be assessed on whether the decisions you make on how to load, filter, process, align, reformat, and save the data are reasonable and match the reference paper and code and these instructions. During testing, the reference version of `/app/train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code. \n\n---\n\n## Conversion Workflow\n\n**IMPORTANT**: Follow these steps in order. Each step has explicit completion criteria. Do not proceed to the next step until the current step is complete. Use the exact step names below when documenting progress in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n**STATUS TRACKING**: At the start of each step, update its status to `IN PROGRESS` in CONVERSION_NOTES.md. When complete, update to `COMPLETE`. This helps you and the user track progress.\n\n---\n\n### Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n\n**Goal**: Set up the working environment and create the notes file.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. **FIRST**: Create `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` with the template structure shown at the end of this document\n2. Verify you can run `python3` and import key packages (numpy, torch)\n3. List the contents of this directory to understand what files are available\n4. **REMINDER**: Do NOT look at any files outside this directory\n\n**Done when**: \n- `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` exists with the proper template structure\n- You have confirmed Python and key packages work\n- You have listed directory contents in CONVERSION_NOTES.md\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 1, verify that `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` exists by running `ls -la /app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. If it doesn't exist, STOP and create it now.\n\n---\n\n### Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n\n**⚠️ CONSTRAINT REMINDER**: Only look at code in the `/app/code` directory. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\n\n**Goal**: Understand how the original authors loaded and processed their data.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Look for documentation or README files in the `/app/code` directory\n- Read the provided reference code bases to see how the reference paper loaded and manipulated the data\n- Understand which functions are being called to read, curate, process, and manipulate the data\n- For neural imaging data, does delta F over F need to be computed? \n- For electrophysiology neural data, do cells need to be filtered based on quality? \n- Note to file important functions, their inputs, and outputs\n\n**Done when**: You have documented key functions and their purposes in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 1\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 2, update Step 1 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n\n**⚠️ CONSTRAINT REMINDER**: Only look at data in the `/app/data` directory. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\n\n**Goal**: Understand the native data structure\n\n**Actions**:\n- Identify how data is organized (all files, formats, hierarchies)\n- Determine available variables and their meanings\n- Check data dimensions and data types\n- Look for documentation or README files\n- Note the data organization and structure\n- Note dataset size parameters such as the **total number** of subjects (mice), sessions, trials, and neurons\n\n**Done when**: You have documented the data structure, file formats, size, and available variables in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 2\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 3, update Step 2 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n\n**Goal**: Extract key information from the paper and methods.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Scan the reference papers and methods.txt for important details about the data\n- Look for and note to file:\n  - Any information about dataset size (number of neurons, subjects, sessions, trials)\n  - Fraction of data with different values, e.g. fraction of rewarded outcomes\n  - Data processing, curation, and filtering\n  - Variables important to experiments\n  - Temporal alignment\n  - Temporal binning\n- Read the reference paper to find information about expected decoder accuracy.\n\n**Done when**: You have documented expected dataset statistics and processing details in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 3\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 4, update Step 3 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 4: Check for Consistency\n\n**Goal**: Verify your understanding is consistent across all sources. \n\n**Actions**:\n- Compare your understanding of:\n  - Important functions from exploring the reference code\n  - Dataset structure from exploring the dataset\n  - Analysis described in the reference texts\n- Note and investigate any discrepancies\n- In particular, look for discrepancies in:\n  - Experimental and behavioral variables\n  - Data processing\n  - Data curation\n- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n\n**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n\n**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Map source data elements to target format components:\n  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `/app/code` directory. \n- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n  - Temporal alignment of trials\n  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n  - Brain regions recorded from\n- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n\n**Done when**:\n- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 6: Script Development\n\n**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `/app/convert_data.py`.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Write all conversion code in the file `/app/convert_data.py`\n- Script should run as **`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n- Include options:\n  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n- Write efficient code:\n  - Vectorize loops\n  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n  - There are no temporal misalignments\n  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n- Write clear, well-documented functions\n- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n\n**Done when**: `/app/convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `/app/convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n\n**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_sample_out.txt`\n  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n3. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_sample_out.txt`.\n4. Analyze the output file `/app/verification_sample_out.txt`:\n  a. Verify no errors reported.\n  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n\n**Done when**: \n- `/app/sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n- `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n- Processing plots show no anomalies\n- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `/app/sample_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n\n---\n\n### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n\n**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n\n**Investigate any issues**:\n- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n\n**Done when**: \n- Decoder training completes without errors\n- Loss decreases over epochs\n- Accuracies are above chance\n- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n\n---\n\n### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n\n**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n3. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_full_out.txt`\n4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n5. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_full_out.txt`. \n6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `/app/verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n\n**Done when**:\n- `/app/converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n- `/app/verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `/app/converted_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n\n---\n\n### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n\n**Check 1: Output log verification**\nRead through `/app/verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n- Document your sanity checks to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n- Fix any mismatches. \n- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n\n**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n- Fix any discovered issues. \n- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\nWrite a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n\n---\n\n### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n\n**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n\n**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \n\n**Done when**: \n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n\n---\n\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\n**Report all checks and results** to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n\n---\n\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n  - Key decisions and rationale\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n  - Validation results and tables\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n- Create a user-facing `/app/README.md` summarizing:\n  - Dataset description\n  - How to load and use the converted data\n  - Output format specification\n  - Key statistics\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `/app/cache/` folder\n- Create `/app/cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n\n---\n\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. \"\"\"\n95\t    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n96\t    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n97\t    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n98\t    one = np.ones(a.shape, dtype=a.dtype)\n99\t    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n100\t f'{path}: F has {F.shape[1]} frames, behavior has {n_frames}'\n289\t    dt = float(np.median(np.diff(beh['time'])))\n290\t    fs = 1.0 / dt\n291\t\n292\t    # ---- trial boundaries -------------------------------------------------------------\n293\t    trial_starts = np.where(beh['trial_start'] > 0)[0]\n294\t    teleports = np.where(beh['teleport'] > 0)[0]\n295\t    # robustness: drop an unpaired leading teleport / trailing trial start\n296\t    if len(teleports) and len(trial_starts) and teleports[0] < trial_starts[0]:\n297\t        teleports = teleports[1:]\n298\t    if len(trial_starts) > len(teleports):\n299\t        trial_starts = trial_starts[:len(teleports)]\n300\t    if len(teleports) > len(trial_starts):\n301\t        teleports = teleports[:len(trial_starts)]\n302\t    assert np.all(teleports > trial_starts), f'{path}: teleport before trial start'\n303\t    n_trials = len(trial_starts)\n304\t\n305\t    # ---- per-trial task variables (behavior.get_trial_types / get_reward_zones) --------\n306\t    reward_frames = np.searchsorted(beh['time'], beh['reward_times'])\n307\t    reward_frames = np.clip(reward_frames, 0, n_frames - 1)\n308\t\n309\t    zone_labels = zones_from_scene(scene, n_trials)\n310\t    zone_starts = np.array([REWARD_ZONE_CM[z][0] for z in zone_labels])\n311\t    zone_stops = np.array([REWARD_ZONE_CM[z][1] for z in zone_labels])\n312\t\n313\t    is_reward = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n314\t    environment = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n315\t    lick_error = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n316\t    for i, (st, sp_) in enumerate(zip(trial_starts, teleports)):\n317\t        # behavior.get_trial_types: reward delivered AND the reward zone was active\n318\t        any_reward = np.any((reward_frames >= st) & (reward_frames < sp_))\n319\t        any_rzone = np.any(beh['reward_zone'][st:sp_] > 0)\n320\t        is_reward[i] = int(bool(any_reward and any_rzone))\n321\t        env_vals = np.unique(beh['environment'][st:sp_])\n322\t        env_vals = env_vals[env_vals >= 0]\n323\t        assert len(env_vals) == 1, f'{path}: trial {i} has environment values {env_vals}'\n324\t        environment[i] = int(env_vals[0])\n325\t        # behavior.correct_lick_sensor_error, threshold from Methods (>30%)\n326\t        seg = beh['lick'][st:sp_]\n327\t        lick_error[i] = (np.sum(seg > LICK_ERROR_COUNT_THRESH) / len(seg)) > LICK_ERROR_FRAC_THRESH\n328\t\n329\t    prev_outcome = np.empty(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n330\t    prev_outcome[1:] = is_reward[:-1]\n331\t    prev_outcome[0] = 1  # see CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5, decision 8\n332\t\n333\t    # ---- dF/F and deconvolved events ---------------------------------------------------\n334\t    keep_teleports = exp_day in KEEP_TELEPORT_DAYS.get(subject, [])\n335\t    t3 = time.time()\n336\t    dff, events = dff_and_events(F, Fneu, trial_starts, teleports, keep_teleports, fs)\n337\t    timing['dff_events'] = time.time() - t3\n338\t\n339\t    # ---- neuron curation: putative interneurons (spatial.is_putative_interneuron) -------\n340\t    valid = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n341\t    speed_valid = beh['speed'][valid]\n342\t    dv = dff[:, valid]\n343\t    dv = dv - dv.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\n344\t    sv = speed_valid - speed_valid.mean()\n345\t    denom = np.sqrt((dv ** 2).sum(axis=1) * (sv ** 2).sum())\n346\t    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):\n347\t        speed_corr = (dv @ sv) / denom\n348\t    is_interneuron = speed_corr > INTERNEURON_R_THRESH\n349\t\n350\t    finite_cells = np.all(np.isfinite(events[:, valid]), axis=1) & np.isfinite(speed_corr)\n351\t    keep_cells = (~is_interneuron) & finite_cells\n352\t    n_dropped_nonfinite = int(np.sum(~finite_cells))\n353\t    events = events[keep_cells]\n354\t    dff_kept = dff[keep_cells]\n355\t    plane_of_cell = plane_of_cell[keep_cells]\n356\t\n357\t    # ---- build per-trial neural / input / output ---------------------------------------\n358\t    neural, inputs, outputs = [], [], []\n359\t    kept_trials = []\n360\t    for i, (st, sp_) in enumerate(zip(trial_starts, teleports)):\n361\t        if lick_error[i]:\n362\t            continue\n363\t        pos = beh['position'][st:sp_]\n364\t        speed = beh['speed'][st:sp_]\n365\t        lick = beh['lick'][st:sp_]\n366\t        tt = beh['time'][st:sp_] - beh['time'][st]\n367\t        T = sp_ - st\n368\t\n369\t        act = events[:, st:sp_]\n370\t        if not np.all(np.isfinite(act)):\n371\t            # should never happen; guard so the decoder never sees NaN\n372\t            act = np.nan_to_num(act, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)\n373\t\n374\t        # distance to the nearest point of the reward zone (0 while inside it)\n375\t        d = np.zeros(T)\n376\t        before = pos < zone_starts[i]\n377\t        after = pos > zone_stops[i]\n378\t        d[before] = pos[before] - zone_starts[i]\n379\t        d[after] = pos[after] - zone_stops[i]\n380\t\n381\t        inp = np.empty((len(INPUT_NAMES), T), dtype=np.float32)\n382\t        inp[0] = tt\n383\t        inp[1] = environment[i]\n384\t        inp[2] = i\n385\t        inp[3] = prev_outcome[i]\n386\t\n387\t        out = np.empty((len(OUTPUT_NAMES), T), dtype=np.int64)\n388\t        out[0] = digitize_distance_to_reward(d)\n389\t        out[1] = digitize_position(pos)\n390\t        out[2] = digitize_speed(speed)\n391\t        out[3] = (lick > 0).astype(np.int64)\n392\t        out[4] = REWARD_ZONE_LABELS.index(zone_labels[i])\n393\t        out[5] = is_reward[i]\n394\t\n395\t        neural.append(np.ascontiguousarray(act, dtype=np.float32))\n396\t        inputs.append(inp)\n397\t        outputs.append(out)\n398\t        kept_trials.append(i)\n399\t\n400\t    result = {\n401\t        'session_key': f'{subject}_ses-{session_id}',\n402\t        'subject': subject,\n403\t        'exp_day': exp_day,\n404\t        'scene': scene,\n405\t        'identifier': identifier,\n406\t        'neural': neural,\n407\t        'input': inputs,\n408\t        'output': outputs,\n409\t        'brain_region_idx': np.zeros(events.shape[0], dtype=np.int64),\n410\t        'n_neurons': int(events.shape[0]),\n411\t        'n_iscell': int(F.shape[0]),\n412\t        'n_interneurons': int(np.sum(is_interneuron)),\n413\t        'n_nonfinite_cells': n_dropped_nonfinite,\n414\t        'n_trials_raw': n_trials,\n415\t        'n_trials_kept': len(neural),\n416\t        'n_lick_error_trials': int(np.sum(lick_error)),\n417\t        'kept_trials': kept_trials,\n418\t        'is_reward': is_reward,\n419\t        'zone_labels': zone_labels.tolist(),\n420\t        'environment': environment,\n421\t        'dt': dt,\n422\t        'imaging_rate': imaging_rate,\n423\t        'n_planes': int(len(np.unique(plane_of_cell))) if len(plane_of_cell) else 1,\n424\t        'region': region,\n425\t        'keep_teleports': bool(keep_teleports),\n426\t        'timing': timing,\n427\t        'total_time': time.time() - t0,\n428\t    }\n429\t\n430\t    if show_processing:\n431\t        plot_processing(result, beh, F, Fneu, dff_kept, events,\n432\t                        trial_starts, teleports, zone_starts, zone_stops, lick_error)\n433\t\n434\t    return result\n435\t\n436\t\n437\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n438\t# Diagnostics\n439\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n440\t\n441\tdef plot_processing(res, beh, F, Fneu, dff, events, trial_starts, teleports,\n442\t                    zone_starts, zone_stops, lick_error):\n443\t    \"\"\"Visualise every processing step for a handful of trials.\"\"\"\n444\t    import matplotlib\n445\t    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n446\t    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n447\t\n448\t    t_i, t_j = 5, 10                      # window of trials to display\n449\t    t_j = min(t_j, len(trial_starts))\n450\t    i0, i1 = trial_starts[t_i], teleports[t_j - 1]\n451\t    sl = slice(i0, i1)\n452\t    tt = beh['time'][sl]\n453\t\n454\t    fig, ax = plt.subplots(8, 1, figsize=(18, 24), sharex=True)\n455\t\n456\t    cells = np.arange(min(4, dff.shape[0]))\n457\t    for c in cells:\n458\t        ax[0].plot(tt, F[c, sl], lw=0.7, label=f'F cell {c}')\n459\t        ax[0].plot(tt, Fneu[c, sl], lw=0.5, ls=':', alpha=0.6)\n460\t    ax[0].set_ylabel('raw F (solid)\\nFneu (dotted)')\n461\t    ax[0].legend(fontsize=7, ncol=4)\n462\t    ax[0].set_title(f\"{res['session_key']}  {res['scene']}  \"\n463\t                    f\"(keep_teleports={res['keep_teleports']}, trials {t_i}-{t_j-1})\")\n464\t\n465\t    for c in cells:\n466\t        ax[1].plot(tt, dff[c, sl], lw=0.8)\n467\t    ax[1].set_ylabel('dF/F\\n(neuropil-sub., maximin,\\nsigma=2 smoothed)')\n468\t\n469\t    for c in cells:\n470\t        ax[2].plot(tt, events[c, sl], lw=0.8)\n471\t    ax[2].set_ylabel('deconvolved events\\n(OASIS)')\n472\t\n473\t    im = ax[3].imshow(events[:min(150, events.shape[0]), sl], aspect='auto',\n474\t                      extent=[tt[0], tt[-1], min(150, events.shape[0]), 0],\n475\t                      vmin=0, vmax=np.nanpercentile(events[:, sl], 99), cmap='magma')\n476\t    ax[3].set_ylabel('events\\n(first 150 cells)')\n477\t\n478\t    pos = beh['position'][sl]\n479\t    ax[4].plot(tt, pos, 'k', lw=1, label='position')\n480\t    for k in range(t_i, t_j):\n481\t        s, e = trial_starts[k], teleports[k]\n482\t        ax[4].plot([beh['time'][s], beh['time'][e - 1]],\n483\t                   [zone_starts[k], zone_starts[k]], 'g-', lw=2)\n484\t        ax[4].plot([beh['time'][s], beh['time'][e - 1]],\n485\t                   [zone_stops[k], zone_stops[k]], 'g-', lw=2)\n486\t    ax[4].set_ylabel('position (cm)\\ngreen = reward zone')\n487\t    ax[4].legend(fontsize=7)\n488\t\n489\t    # discretised outputs, reassembled from the exported trials for this window\n490\t    keyed = {k: n for n, k in enumerate(res['kept_trials'])}\n491\t    for k in range(t_i, t_j):\n492\t        if k not in keyed:\n493\t            continue\n494\t        n = keyed[k]\n495\t        tk = beh['time'][trial_starts[k]:teleports[k]]\n496\t        out = res['output'][n]\n497\t        ax[5].step(tk, out[0], where='post', color='C0')\n498\t        ax[5].step(tk, out[1], where='post', color='C1', alpha=0.7)\n499\t        ax[6].step(tk, out[2], where='post', color='C2')\n500\t        ax[6].step(tk, out[3] * 4, where='post', color='C3', alpha=0.6)\n501\t        inp = res['input'][n]\n502\t        ax[7].plot(tk, inp[0], color='C4')\n503\t        ax[7].plot(tk, inp[1] * 10, color='C5')\n504\t        ax[7].plot(tk, inp[3] * 5, color='C6')\n505\t    ax[5].set_ylabel('out0 dist-to-zone (C0)\\nout1 position bin (C1)')\n506\t    ax[6].set_ylabel('out2 speed bin (C2)\\nout3 lick x4 (C3)')\n507\t    ax[7].set_ylabel('in0 t-from-start (C4)\\nin1 env x10 (C5)\\nin3 prev-rew x5 (C6)')\n508\t    ax[7].set_xlabel('session time (s)')\n509\t\n510\t    for a in ax:\n511\t        for k in range(t_i, t_j):\n512\t            a.axvline(beh['time'][trial_starts[k]], color='g', lw=0.8, alpha=0.6)\n513\t            a.axvline(beh['time'][teleports[k]], color='r', lw=0.8, alpha=0.6)\n514\t\n515\t    fig.tight_layout()\n516\t    fname = f\"processing_{res['session_key']}.png\"\n517\t    fig.savefig(fname, dpi=110)\n518\t    plt.close(fig)\n519\t    print(f'  wrote {fname}', flush=True)\n520\t\n521\t    # --- second figure: verification of the discretisations against the raw streams -----\n522\t    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(18, 5))\n523\t    allpos, alld, allspeed = [], [], []\n524\t    for n, k in enumerate(res['kept_trials']):\n525\t        s, e = trial_starts[k], teleports[k]\n526\t        p = beh['position'][s:e]\n527\t        allpos.append(p)\n528\t        d = np.zeros(len(p))\n529\t        d[p < zone_starts[k]] = p[p < zone_starts[k]] - zone_starts[k]\n530\t        d[p > zone_stops[k]] = p[p > zone_stops[k]] - zone_stops[k]\n531\t        alld.append(d)\n532\t        allspeed.append(beh['speed'][s:e])\n533\t    allpos = np.concatenate(allpos)\n534\t    alld = np.concatenate(alld)\n535\t    allspeed = np.concatenate(allspeed)\n536\t    allout = np.concatenate(res['output'], axis=1)\n537\t    ax[0].scatter(alld, allout[0], s=1, alpha=0.2)\n538\t    ax[0].set_xlabel('distance to reward zone (cm)'); ax[0].set_ylabel('output 0 bin')\n539\t    ax[1].scatter(allpos, allout[1], s=1, alpha=0.2)\n540\t    ax[1].set_xlabel('position (cm)'); ax[1].set_ylabel('output 1 bin')\n541\t    ax[2].scatter(allspeed, allout[2], s=1, alpha=0.2)\n542\t    ax[2].set_xlabel('speed (cm/s)'); ax[2].set_ylabel('output 2 bin')\n543\t    ax[2].set_xlim(-10, 80)\n544\t    fig.suptitle(f\"{res['session_key']}: discretisation check\")\n545\t    fig.tight_layout()\n546\t    fname = f\"processing_{res['session_key']}_discretisation.png\"\n547\t    fig.savefig(fname, dpi=110)\n548\t    plt.close(fig)\n549\t    print(f'  wrote {fname}', flush=True)\n550\t\n551\t\n552\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n553\t# Driver\n554\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n555\t\n556\tdef list_sessions():\n557\t    files = []\n558\t    for sub in sorted(os.listdir(DATA_ROOT)):\n559\t        d = os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, sub)\n560\t        if not os.path.isdir(d):\n561\t            continue\n562\t        for fn in sorted(os.listdir(d)):\n563\t            if fn.endswith('.nwb'):\n564\t                files.append(os.path.join(d, fn))\n565\t    # deterministic order: subject number, then session number\n566\t    def key(p):\n567\t        base = os.path.basename(p)\n568\t        sub = base.split('_')[0].replace('sub-m', '')\n569\t        ses = base.split('_')[1].replace('ses-', '')\n570\t        return (int(sub), int(ses))\n571\t    return sorted(files, key=key)\n572\t\n573\t\n574\tdef _worker(args):\n575\t    path, show = args\n576\t    warnings.simplefilter('ignore')\n577\t    try:\n578\t        return process_session(path, show_processing=show)\n579\t    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n580\t        import traceback\n581\t        traceback.print_exc()\n582\t        return {'error': f'{path}: {exc}'}\n583\t\n584\t\n585\tdef main():\n586\t    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,\n587\t                                     formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)\n588\t    parser.add_argument('outfile', type=str)\n589\t    parser.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', help='process all sessions (default)')\n590\t    parser.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='process only 2 sessions')\n591\t    parser.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true',\n592\t                        help='save per-step plots for up to 2 sessions')\n593\t    parser.add_argument('--workers', type=int, default=None)\n594\t    args = parser.parse_args()\n595\t\n596\t    files = list_sessions()\n597\t    if args.sample:\n598\t        # one single-plane and one two-plane session, one with keep_teleports=True\n599\t        picks = ['sub-m11/sub-m11_ses-03_behavior+ophys.nwb',\n600\t                 'sub-m17/sub-m17_ses-01_behavior+ophys.nwb']\n601\t        files = [os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, p) for p in picks]\n602\t    print(f'Processing {len(files)} sessions', flush=True)\n603\t\n604\t    n_show = 2 if args.show_processing else 0\n605\t    jobs = [(p, i < n_show) for i, p in enumerate(files)]\n606\t\n607\t    workers = args.workers\n608\t    if workers is None:\n609\t        workers = min(10, len(files), max(1, os.cpu_count() // 4))\n610\t\n611\t    t_start = time.time()\n612\t    results = []\n613\t    if workers <= 1 or len(files) == 1:\n614\t        for j in jobs:\n615\t            results.append(_worker(j))\n616\t            r = results[-1]\n617\t            print(f\"[{len(results)}/{len(files)}] {r.get('session_key', r.get('error'))} \"\n618\t                  f\"{r.get('total_time', 0):.1f}s\", flush=True)\n619\t    else:\n620\t        import multiprocessing as mp\n621\t        ctx = mp.get_context('spawn')\n622\t        with ctx.Pool(workers, maxtasksperchild=2) as pool:\n623\t            for r in pool.imap(_worker, jobs):\n624\t                results.append(r)\n625\t                elapsed = time.time() - t_start\n626\t                rate = elapsed / len(results)\n627\t                print(f\"[{len(results)}/{len(files)}] {r.get('session_key', r.get('error'))} \"\n628\t                      f\"cells={r.get('n_neurons')} trials={r.get('n_trials_kept')} \"\n629\t                      f\"t={r.get('total_time', 0):.1f}s \"\n630\t                      f\"| elapsed {elapsed/60:.1f} min, ETA {(len(files)-len(results))*rate/60:.1f} min\",\n631\t                      flush=True)\n632\t\n633\t    errors = [r for r in results if 'error' in r]\n634\t    if errors:\n635\t        for e in errors:\n636\t            print('ERROR:', e['error'])\n637\t        raise SystemExit('conversion failed')\n638\t\n639\t    results.sort(key=lambda r: (int(r['subject'][1:]), r['exp_day']))\n640\t\n641\t    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results}, key=lambda s: int(s[1:]))\n642\t    sub_index = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n643\t\n644\t    data = {\n645\t        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n646\t        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n647\t        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n648\t        'subjects': subjects,\n649\t        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_index[r['subject']] for r in results], dtype=np.int64),\n650\t        'brain_regions': ['CA1'],\n651\t        'brain_region_idx': [r['brain_region_idx'] for r in results],\n652\t        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n653\t        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n654\t        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n655\t    }\n656\t\n657\t    dts = np.array([r['dt'] for r in results])\n658\t    n_trials = sum(r['n_trials_kept'] for r in results)\n659\t    n_neurons = sum(r['n_neurons'] for r in results)\n660\t\n661\t    data['metadata'] = {\n662\t        'task_description':\n663\t            'Head-fixed mice run laps on a 450 cm virtual linear track (ENV 1 or ENV 2) with a '\n664\t            'hidden 50 cm reward zone at one of three locations (A: 80-130, B: 200-250, '\n665\t            'C: 320-370 cm). Licking inside the zone delivers sucrose water; reward is randomly '\n666\t            'omitted on ~15% of trials. On switch sessions the zone moves to a new location after '\n667\t            'trial 30 (0-indexed trial 30), sometimes together with a switch of environment. '\n668\t            'Decoded outputs are the signed distance to the reward zone, absolute track position, '\n669\t            'running speed, licking, the active reward-zone location, and whether the trial was '\n670\t            'rewarded.',\n671\t        'time_bin_size': float(np.median(dts) * 1000.0),\n672\t        'time_bin_size_range_ms': [float(dts.min() * 1000), float(dts.max() * 1000)],\n673\t        'temporal_alignment_event':\n674\t            'trial start (entry to the linear track at position 0 cm, NWB \"trial_start\" event)',\n675\t        'off_start': 0.0,\n676\t        'off_end': None,\n677\t        'trial_window':\n678\t            'each trial spans [trial_start, teleport), i.e. the whole on-track lap; laps have '\n679\t            'variable duration (median ~12.2 s) so off_end is not a fixed value',\n680\t        'neural_signal':\n681\t            'deconvolved calcium activity (\"events\"): raw suite2p F, neuropil-subtracted '\n682\t            '(coef 0.7), per-trial maximin dF/F baseline (Gaussian sigma=15 frames, then '\n683\t            'min/max filter over 300 frames ~20 s), dF/F = (F-b)/|b|, smoothed with a '\n684\t            '2-frame s.d. Gaussian, then OASIS deconvolution (tau=0.7) - exactly '\n685\t            'reward_relative.preprocessing.dff(neuropil_method=\"subtract\", '\n686\t            'baseline_method=\"maximin\", deconvolve=True)',\n687\t        'neuron_curation':\n688\t            'suite2p manual curation (iscell==1), then putative interneurons removed '\n689\t            '(Pearson r(dF/F, speed) > 0.5)',\n690\t        'trial_curation':\n691\t            'trials with a lick-sensor error (>30% of frames with cumulative lick count > 2) '\n692\t            'are removed, as in the paper Methods',\n693\t        'source': 'DANDI:001361 - Sosa, Plitt & Giocomo 2025, Nature Neuroscience',\n694\t        'brain_region_detail': 'dorsal hippocampus CA1 (2-photon, GCaMP7f); imaging planes pooled',\n695\t        'input_descriptions': [\n696\t            'time since trial start (s)',\n697\t            'environment: 0 = ENV 1, 1 = ENV 2 (constant within a trial)',\n698\t            'trial number within the session (0-indexed, constant within a trial)',\n699\t            'outcome of the previous trial: 0 = omitted, 1 = rewarded; the first trial of each '\n700\t            'session is set to 1 (undefined; the modal outcome)',\n701\t        ],\n702\t        'output_descriptions': [\n703\t            'signed distance (cm) to the nearest point of the active reward zone, 0 inside repository\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef nansmooth(a, sig, axis=-1):\n    \"\"\"Gaussian smoothing of an array containing NaNs without propagating them.\n\n    Verbatim port of `reward_relative.utilities.nansmooth` (same implementation as\n    `TwoPUtils.utilities.nansmooth`, which `preprocessing.dff` imports).\n    \"\"\"\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n    one = np.ones(a.shape, dtype=a.dtype)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    a_nanless = sp.ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis)\n    one = sp.ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\n    return a_nanless / one\n\n\ndef zones_from_scene(scene, n_trials, change_trial=CHANGE_TRIAL):\n    \"\"\"Reward-zone label ('A'/'B'/'C') for each trial, from the VR scene name.\n\n    Port of `behavior.get_reward_zones` for the scenes present in this dataset:\n    'Env1_LocationB', 'Env1_LocationB_to_A', 'Env1_B_to_Env2_C', ...\n    \"\"\"\n    for first in REWARD_ZONE_LABELS:\n        if f'{first}_to' in scene:\n            second = scene[-1]\n            if NotImplementedError(f'Reward zone not defined for scene {scene!r}')\n\n\ndef dff_and_events(F, Fneu, trial_starts, teleports, keep_teleports,\n                   fs, neu_coef=NEU_COEF, tau=TAU):\n    \"\"\"deltaF/F and deconvolved 'events' for one session.\n\n    Faithful port of `reward_relative.preprocessing.dff` with\n    ``neuropil_method='subtract'``, ``baseline_method='maximin'``,\n    ``subtract_baseline=True``, ``deconvolve=True``.\n\n    The one intentional difference is the trial slicing convention: the reference slices\n    ``[start-1:stop-1]``; here we slice ``[start:stop]`` so that the kept samples are exactly\n    the on-track frames of the lap (the frame at the teleport index carries a corrupted,\n    interpolated position and is excluded by both conventions). See CONVERSION_NOTES Step 4.\n\n    Args:\n        F: (n_cells, n_frames) raw suite2p fluorescence.\n        Fneu: (n_cells, n_frames) neuropil fluorescence.\n        trial_starts, teleports: 1).tolist()\n        stop_inds = np.asarray(teleports).tolist()\n    else:\n        start_inds = np.asarray(trial_starts).tolist()\n        stop_inds = np.asarray(teleports).tolist()\n\n    f_ = independently within each segment\n    flow = np.full(f_.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    for start, stop in zip(start_inds, stop_inds):\n        seg = f_[:, start:stop]\n        # add back the per-trial neuropil mean so dF/F is close to true deltaF/F\n        seg += neu_coef * np.nanmean(f_neu_[:, start:stop], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n        f_[:, start:stop] = seg\n        base = nansmooth(seg, BASELINE_SMOOTH_SIGMA, axis=-1)\n        base = sp.ndimage.minimum_filter1d(base, BASELINE_FILTER_WIN, axis=-1)\n        base = sp.ndimage.maximum_filter1d(base, BASELINE_FILTER_WIN, axis=-1)\n        flow[:, start:stop] = base\n\n    dff = np.full(f_.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n\n start:stop] = smoothed\n        events[:, start:stop] = dcnv.oasis(np.ascontiguousarray(smoothed), 2000, tau, fs)\n\n    return dff, events\n\n\ndef digitize_distance_to_reward(d):\n    \"\"\"Discretise signed distance (cm) to the nearest point of the reward zone.\n\n    0: < -50 | 1: [-50,-10) | 2: [-10,0) | 3: == 0 (inside the zone)\n    4: (0,10] | 5: (10,50] | 6: > 50\n    \"\"\"\n    out = np.empty(d.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n    out[d < -50] = 0\n    out[(d >= -50) & (d < -10)] = 1\n    out[(d >= -10) & (d < 0)] = 2\n    out[d == 0] = 3\n    out[(d > 0) & (d <= 10)] = 4\n    out[(d > 10) & (d <= 50)] = 5\n    out[d > 50] = 6\n    return out\n\n\ndef digitize_position(pos):\n    \"\"\"5 equal 90 cm bins over the 450 cm track.\"\"\"\n    return np.clip((pos // 90.0), 0, 4).astype(np.int64)\n\n\ndef digitize_speed(speed):\n    \"\"\"<2, 2-10, 10-20, 20-40, >40 cm/s.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(speed, [2.0, 10.0, 20.0, 40.0]).astype(np.int64)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-session conversion\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef load_behavior(f):\n    \"\"\"Read every behaviour stream (already on the imaging-frame grid) from an open NWB file.\"\"\"\n    b = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries']\n    beh = {\n        'time': b['position/timestamps'][:],\n        'position': b['position/data'][:],\n        'speed': b['speed/data'][:],\n        'lick': b['lick/data'][:],\n        'environment': b['environment/data'][:],\n        'reward_zone': b['reward_zone/data'][:],\n        'trial_number': b['trial b['Reward/timestamps'][:],\n    }\n    return beh\n\n\ndef load_fluorescence(f):\n    \"\"\"Concatenate planes and keep only manually curated cells (`iscell==1`).\n\n    Mirrors TwoPUtils' `load_suite2p_data`, which keeps `iscell[:, 0].astype(bool)`.\n    Planes are pooled, as the paper does for all analyses except Extended Data Fig. 7.\n    \"\"\"\n    seg = f['processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation']\n    iscell = seg['iscell'][:, 0].astype(bool)\n    plane_idx = seg['planeIdx'][:]\n\n    Fs, Fneus, keep = [], [], []\n    offset = 0\n    for plane in sorted(f['processing/ophys/Fluorescence'].keys()):\n        Fp = f[f'processing/ophys/Fluorescence/{plane}/data']\n        Np = f[f'processing/ophys/Neuropil/{plane}/data']\n        n_roi = Fp.shape[1]\n        sel = iscell[offset:offset + n_roi]\n        # read then subset: column fancy-indexing through hdf5 is far slower\n        Fs.append(np.asarray(Fp[:, :], dtype=np.float32)[:, sel].T)\n        Fneus.append(np.asarray(Np[:, :], dtype=np.float32)[:, sel].T)\n        keep.append(plane_idx[offset:offset + n_roi][sel])\n        offset += n_roi\n    assert offset == len(iscell), 'plane ROI counts do not cover PlaneSegmentation'\n    return np.concatenate(Fs, axis=0), np.concatenate(Fneus, axis=0), np.concatenate(keep)\n\n\ndef process_session(path, show_processing=False):\n    \"\"\"Convert one NWB session. Returns a dict (or raises).\"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n    timing = {}\n    with h5py.File(path, 'r') as f:\n        subject = f['general/subject/subject_id'][()].decode()\n        session_id = f['general/session_id'][()].decode()\n        exp_day = int(session_id)\n        identifier = f['identifier'][()].decode()\n        scene = identifier.split('/')[-1]\n        imaging_rate = float(f['general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/imaging_rate'][()])\n        region = f['general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/location'][()].decode()\n\n        beh = load_behavior(f)\n        t1 = time.time(); timing['read_behavior'] = t1 - t0\n        F, Fneu, plane_of_cell = load_fluorescence(f)\n        t2 = time.time(); timing['read_fluorescence'] = t2 - t1\n\n    n_frames = len(beh['time'])\n    assert F.shape[1] == n_frames, f'{path}: F has {F.shape[1]} frames, behavior has {n_frames}'\n    dt = float(np.median(np.diff(beh['time'])))\n    fs = 1.0 / dt\n\n    # ---- trial boundaries -------------------------------------------------------------\n    trial_starts = np.where(beh['trial_start'] > 0)[0]\n    teleports = np.where(beh['teleport'] > 0)[0]\n    # robustness: drop an unpaired leading teleport / trailing trial start\n    if len(teleports) and len(trial_starts) and teleports[0] < trial_starts[0]:\n        teleports = teleports[1:]\n    if len(trial_starts) > len(teleports):\n        trial_starts = trial_starts[:len(teleports)]\n    if len(teleports) > len(trial_starts):\n        teleports = teleports[:len(trial_starts)]\n    assert np.all(teleports > trial_starts), f'{path}: teleport before trial start'\n    n_trials = len(trial_starts)\n\n    # ---- per-trial task variables (behavior.get_trial_types / get_reward_zones) --------\n    reward_frames = np.searchsorted(beh['time'], beh['reward_times'])\n    reward_frames = np.clip(reward_frames, 0, n_frames - 1)\n\n    zone_labels = zones_from_scene(scene, n_trials)\n    zone_starts = np.array([REWARD_ZONE_CM[z][0] for z in zone_labels])\n    zone_stops = np.array([REWARD_ZONE_CM[z][1] for z in zone_labels])\n\n    is_reward = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n    environment = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n    lick_error = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n    for i, (st, sp_) in enumerate(zip(trial_starts, teleports)):\n        # behavior.get_trial_types: reward delivered AND the reward zone was active\n        any_reward = np.any((reward_frames >= st) & (reward_frames < sp_))\n        any_rzone = np.any(beh['reward_zone'][st:sp_] > 0)\n        is_reward[i] = int(bool(any_reward and any_rzone))\n        env_vals = np.unique(beh['environment'][st:sp_])\n        env_vals = env_vals[env_vals >= 0]\n        assert len(env_vals) == 1, f'{path}: trial {i} has environment values {env_vals}'\n        environment[i] = int(env_vals[0])\n        # behavior.correct_lick_sensor_error, threshold from Methods (>30%)\n        seg = beh['lick'][st:sp_]\n        lick_error[i] = (np.sum(seg > LICK_ERROR_COUNT_THRESH) / len(seg)) > LICK_ERROR_FRAC_THRESH\n\n    prev_outcome = np.empty(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n    prev_outcome[1:] = is_reward[:-1]\n    prev_outcome[0] = 1  # see CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5, decision 8\n\n    # ---- dF/F and deconvolved events ---------------------------------------------------\n    keep_teleports = exp_day in KEEP_TELEPORT_DAYS.get(subject, [])\n    t3 = time.time()\n    dff, events = dff_and_events(F, Fneu, trial_starts, teleports, keep_teleports, fs)\n    timing['dff_events'] = time.time() - dv = dv - dv.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\n    sv = speed_valid - speed_valid.mean()\n    denom = np.sqrt((dv ** 2).sum(axis=1) * (sv ** 2).sum())\n    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):\n        speed_corr = (dv @ sv) / denom\n    is_interneuron = speed_corr > INTERNEURON_R_THRESH\n\n    finite_cells = np.all(np.isfinite(events[:, valid]), axis=1) & np.isfinite(speed_corr)\n    keep_cells = (~is_interneuron) & finite_cells\n    n_dropped_nonfinite = int(np.sum(~finite_cells))\n    events = events[keep_cells]\n    dff_kept = dff[keep_cells]\n    plane_of_cell = plane_of_cell[keep_cells]\n\n    # ---- build per-trial neural / input / output ---------------------------------------\n    neural, inputs, outputs = [], [], []\n    kept_trials = []\n    for i, (st, sp_) in enumerate(zip(trial_starts, teleports)):\n        if lick_error[i]:\n            continue\n        pos = beh['position'][st:sp_]\n        speed = beh['speed'][st:sp_]\n        lick = beh['lick'][st:sp_]\n        tt = beh['time'][st:sp_] - beh['time'][st]\n        T = sp_ - st\n\n        act = events[:, st:sp_]\n        if not np.all(np.isfinite(act)):\n            # should never happen; guard so the decoder never sees NaN\n            act = np.nan_to_num(act, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)\n\n        # distance to the nearest point of the reward zone (0 while inside it)\n        d = np.zeros(T)\n        before = pos < zone_starts[i]\n        after = pos > zone_stops[i]\n        d[before] = pos[before] - zone_starts[i]\n        d[after] = pos[after] - zone_stops[i]\n\n        inp = np.empty((len(INPUT_NAMES), T), dtype=np.float32)\n        inp[0] = tt\n        inp[1] = environment[i]\n        inp[2] = i\n        inp[3] = prev_outcome[i]\n\n        out = np.empty((len(OUTPUT_NAMES), T), dtype=np.int64)\n        out[0] = digitize_distance_to_reward(d)\n        out[1] = digitize_position(pos)\n        out[2] = digitize_speed(speed)\n        out[3] = (lick > 0).astype(np.int64)\n        out[4] = REWARD_ZONE_LABELS.index(zone_labels[i])\n        out[5] = is_reward[i]\n\n        neural.append(np.ascontiguousarray(act, dtype=np.float32))\n        inputs.append(inp)\n        outputs.append(out)\n        kept_trials.append(i)\n\n    result = {\n        'session_key': f'{subject}_ses-{session_id}',\n        'subject': subject,\n        'exp_day': np.zeros(events.shape[0], dtype=np.int64),\n        'n_neurons': int(events.shape[0]),\n        'n_iscell': int(F.shape[0]),\n        'n_interneurons': int(np.sum(is_interneuron)),\n        'n_nonfinite_cells': n_dropped_nonfinite,\n        'n_trials_raw': n_trials,\n        'n_trials_kept': len(neural),\n        'n_lick_error_trials': int(np.sum(lick_error)),\n        'kept_trials': Animals missing from this table (m3, m4 and m7) were\n68\t# blanked on every day, so their baselines are always restricted to the lap.\n69\t# ** copied from src/reward_relative/teleport_metadata.py::teleport_sessions **\n70\t\n71\t# Dictionary of animals and experiment days for which the teleport periods were imaged\n72\t# (as opposed to blanking the laser)\n73\t\n74\tteleport_sessions = {\n75\t    'GCAMP10': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n76\t    'GCAMP11': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n77\t    'GCAMP12': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n78\t    'GCAMP13': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n79\t    'GCAMP14': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n80\t    'GCAMP15': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n81\t    'GCAMP17': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n82\t    'GCAMP18': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n83\t    'GCAMP19': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n84\t}\n85\t\n86\t\n87\tdef nansmooth(a, sig):\n88\t    \"\"\"\n89\t    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n90\t    without propagating nans\n91\t    :param a:\n92\t    :param sig:\n93\t    :return:\n94\t\n95\t    ** copied from TwoPUtils/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n96\t    \"\"\"\n97\t\n98\t    # find nans\n99\t    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n100\t    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n101\t    # make nans 0\n102\t    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n103\t\n104\t    # inversely weight nanned indices\n105\t    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n106\t    one[nan_inds] = .001\n107\t    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n108\t    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n109\t    return a_nanless / one\n110\t\n111\t\n112\tdef nansmooth_1d(a, sig, axis=-1, return_nans=False, **kwargs):\n113\t    \"\"\"\n114\t    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n115\t    without propagating nans\n116\t    :param a:\n117\t    :param sig:\n118\t    :return:\n119\t\n120\t    ** copied from src/reward_relative/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n121\t    (renamed: the TwoPUtils N-D version above already occupies the name `nansmooth`)\n122\t    \"\"\"\n123\t\n124\t    # find nans\n125\t    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n126\t    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n127\t    # make nans 0\n128\t    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n129\t\n130\t    # inversely weight nanned indices\n131\t    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n132\t    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n133\t    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n134\t    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n135\t\n136\t    out = a_nanless / one\n137\t    if return_nans:\n138\t        out[nan_inds] = np.nan\n139\t\n140\t    return out\n141\t\n142\tdef dff(f,\n143\t        trial_starts,\n144\t        teleports,\n145\t        f_neu=None,\n146\t        neuropil_method=None,\n147\t        baseline_method='maximin',\n148\t        subtract_baseline=True,\n149\t        neu_coef=0.7,\n150\t        tau=0.7,\n151\t        frame_rate=15,\n152\t        n_planes=1,\n153\t        deconvolve=False,\n154\t        keep_teleports=False):\n155\t    \"\"\"\n156\t    Calculate dFF for 1 channel\n157\t\n158\t    :param f: ROI fluorescence timeseries\n159\t    :param trial_starts:\n160\t    :param teleports:\n161\t    :param f_neu: neuropil fluorescence timeseries\n162\t    :param baseline_method:\n163\t    :param subtract_baseline: True gives ∆F/F, False just normalizes F to the basline (divides only)\n164\t    :param neu_coef:\n165\t    :param deconvolve: whether to deconvolve the dff trace into \"events\" (psuedo spikes)\n166\t    :return: dff and spks if deconvolve is True, otherwise just dff\n167\t\n168\t    `trial_starts` and `teleports` must be pandas Series: the keep_teleports branch calls\n169\t    `.to_list()` on `teleports`, which a numpy array does not have. That is their code, unchanged.\n170\t\n171\t    ** copied from src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff **\n172\t    Keeps only the branches this dataset can reach (single-channel)\n173\t    Dropped: bleedthrough_ts, neu_bleedthrough_ts, regress_ts, scrub_ts, neuropil_method='regress',\n174\t    baseline_method='maxsmooth', subtract_baseline=False. \n175\t    \"\"\"\n176\t\n177\t    print('baseline method =', baseline_method)\n178\t\n179\t    # Check frame rate if deconvolving\n180\t    if deconvolve:\n181\t        if (frame_rate/n_planes) > 16:\n182\t            warnings.warn(\n183\t                f\"Using frame rate per plane: {(frame_rate/n_planes)} -- is this correct?\")\n184\t\n185\t    f_ = np.zeros(f.shape) * np.nan\n186\t\n187\t    if f_neu is not None:\n188\t        f_neu_ = np.zeros(f_neu.shape) * np.nan\n189\t\n190\t    if keep_teleports:\n191\t        # if keeping the teleports, keep all the fluorescence from the start of imaging\n192\t        # EXCEPT the teleport sample itself, as this position values might be interpolated between\n193\t        # the end of the track and -50 (where the teleport jitter starts)\n194\t        print(\"keeping teleports\")\n195\t        start_inds = np.append(\n196\t            trial_starts[0],\n197\t            np.array(teleports[:-1] + 2)\n198\t        ).tolist()\n199\t        # ^ add 2 so with start-1 we will start 1 sample after the teleport\n200\t        stop_inds = teleports.to_list()\n201\t        # we will subtract 1 off this index below\n202\t    else:\n203\t        # if not keeping the teleports, keep only the fluorescence on each trial\n204\t        start_inds = trial_starts.tolist()\n205\t        stop_inds = teleports.tolist()\n206\t\n207\t    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n208\t        f_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n209\t\n210\t        if f_neu is not None:\n211\t            f_neu_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f_neu[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n212\t\n213\t    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n214\t\n215\t    # once bleedthrough is subtracted, do neuropil correction\n216\t\n217\t    if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n218\t        f_ -= neu_coef * f_neu_\n219\t\n220\t    print('No minimum subtraction')\n221\t\n222\t    # Caluclate baseline for chan 1\n223\t    flow = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n224\t    spks = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n225\t\n226\t    # get baseline within each trial\n227\t    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n228\t\n229\t        # if we subtracted neuropil, add back in the neuropil mean in each trial\n230\t        # such that dff values will be close to true ∆F/F, and we don't divide by small numbers\n231\t        if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n232\t            f_[:, start-1:stop-1] = f_[:, start-1:stop-1] + neu_coef * np.nanmean(\n233\t                f_neu_[:, start-1:stop-1], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n234\t\n235\t        if baseline_method == 'maximin':\n236\t            # cut out ITIs and smooth signal\n237\t            flow[:, start - 1:stop -\n238\t                 1] = nansmooth(f_[:, start-1:stop-1], [0, 15])\n239\t\n240\t            # minimum filter, taking min val over 20 sec\n241\t            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(\n242\t                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1) # was sp.ndimage.filters.minimum_filter1d\n243\t            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(\n244\t                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1)  # max filter with same window (dilation), was sp.ndimage.filters.maximum_filter1d\n245\t\n246\t    # to get deltaF/F: subtract baseline from initial signal, divide by abs(baseline)\n247\t    # baseline can some times end up as negative due to regression\n248\t    dff = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n249\t    if subtract_baseline:\n250\t        dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]\n251\t                           ) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n252\t\n253\t    print('dff:', dff.shape, 'min ', np.nanmin(dff), 'max ', np.nanmax(dff))\n254\t\n255\t    # Smooth the deltaF/F transients by 2 time bins\n256\t    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n257\t        dff[:, start - 1:stop -\n258\t            1] = nansmooth_1d(dff[:, start - 1:stop - 1], 2, axis=1)\n259\t        # After smoothing: Get \"spikes\" with calcium kernel deconvolution from suite2p\n260\t        if deconvolve:\n261\t            spks[:, start-1:stop-1] = dcnv.oasis(\n262\t                np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, start-1:stop-1], dtype=np.float32),\n263\t                2000, tau, (frame_rate/n_planes)) # this is float64 in the original code\n264\t\n265\t    if deconvolve:\n266\t        return dff, spks\n267\t    else:\n268\t        return dff\n269\t\n270\t\n271\tdef is_putative_interneuron(dff, speed, r_thresh=0.5):\n272\t    \"\"\"\n273\t    Find putative interneurons based on correlation of dF/F with speed\n274\t    (under the hypothesis that fast-spiking interneurons in the pyramidal\n275\t    layer are often highly correlated with running speed).\n276\t\n277\t    :param dff: (ncells, T) dF/F timeseries, NaN outside the analysed windows\n278\t    :param speed: (T,) running speed in cm/s\n279\t    :param r_thresh: threshold for speed correlation r; dayData sets int_thresh = 0.5, which is\n280\t        the value the Methods quote (\"a Pearson correlation of >0.5\"). Note that the threshold\n281\t        defaulted to 0.3 in the source function; the analyses always pass 0.5.\n282\t    :return: is_int (array) with a Boolean for each ROI, where putative interneuron is True.\n283\t\n284\t    ** copied from src/reward_relative/spatial.py::is_putative_interneuron **\n285\t    Change: takes the dF/F and speed arrays as arguments instead of reading them off a TwoPUtils \n286\t    `sess` object, which this conversion does not build.\n287\t    \"\"\"\n288\t    speed_corr = np.zeros((dff.shape[0],))\n289\t    nanmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n290\t\n291\t    for c in range(dff.shape[0]):\n292\t        speed_corr[c] = np.corrcoef(\n293\t            dff[c, nanmask], speed[nanmask])[0, 1]\n294\t\n295\t    is_int = speed_corr > r_thresh\n296\t\n297\t    return is_int\n298\t\n299\t# end copy-paste\n300\t# =================================================================================================\n301\t\n302\t\n303\tdef get_subjects(datadir=DATADIR):\n304\t    # ms\n354\t\n355\t        behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n356\t        istrial = behavior_data['trial number'].data[:] >= 0\n357\t        # reward_amounts are all the same\n358\t        reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:]\n359\t        info['unique_reward_amounts'] = np.unique(reward_amounts)\n360\t        info['nrewards'] = len(reward_amounts)\n361\t        # autoreward is all 0s, so not useful for modeling, but check that it is all zeros\n362\t        autoreward = behavior_data['autoreward'].data[:]\n363\t        info['unique_autoreward'] = np.unique(autoreward[istrial])\n364\t        # replace infinity with the max/min of the data for plotting purposes\n401\t    bin_edges_plot = np.array(bin_edges.copy())\n402\t    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[0]):\n403\t        bin_edges_plot[0] = data.min()\n404\t        if bin_edges_plot[0] > bin_edges_plot[1]:\n405\t            bin_edges_plot[0] = 2*bin_edges_plot[1] - bin_edges_plot[2] # extrapolate to get a lower value for the first bin edge\n406\t    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[-1]):\n407\t        bin_edges_plot[-1] = data.max()\n408\t        if bin_edges_plot[-1] < bin_edges_plot[-2]:\n409\t            bin_edges_plot[-1] = 2*bin_edges_plot[-2] - bin_edges_plot[-3] # extrapolate to get a higher value for the last bin edge\n410\t    bin_centers = (bin_edges_plot[:-1] + bin_edges_plot[1:]) / 2\n411\t    return bin_centers\n412\t    \n413\tdef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n414\t    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n415\t    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n416\t    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n417\t    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n418\t    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0] # negative if before the zone\n419\t    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n420\t    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1] # positive if after the zone\n421\t    return distance_to_reward_zone\n422\t    \n423\tdef convert_session_data(subject, session, info, trial_offset, time_bin_size=None, datadir=DATADIR, \n424\t                         show_processing=False, max_neurons_plot=30, min_ntimepoints=50,\n425\t                         figfile=None):\n426\t\n427\t    nwb_file_path = get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir)\n428\t    io = pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_file_path, 'r')\n429\t    nwbfile = io.read()\n430\t\n431\t    # The deconvolution needs the trial boundaries, so it runs further\n433\t    # below, once those have been parsed out of the behavior data.\n434\t    fluorescence_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Fluorescence\"]\n435\t    neuropil_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Neuropil\"]\n436\t    fluorescence = []\n437\t    neuropil = []\n438\t    nplanes = len(fluorescence_data.roi_response_series)\n439\t    for plane_name, plane_data in fluorescence_data.roi_response_series.items():\n440\t        roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n441\t        iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n442\t        fluorescence_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n443\t        neuropil_curr = neuropil_data.roi_response_series[plane_name].data[:]\n444\t        assert fluorescence_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({fluorescence_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n445\t        assert neuropil_curr.shape == fluorescence_curr.shape, f\"Neuropil shape ({neuropil_curr.shape}) does not match fluorescence shape ({fluorescence_curr.shape})\"\n446\t        # Subset to the curated cells now rather than after dff. Every step of dff is per-cell --\n447\t        # its only cross-cell operation is a Gaussian with sigma 0 on the cell axis -- so this is\n448\t        # numerically identical to subsetting afterwards, on a third of the memory.\n449\t        fluorescence.append(fluorescence_curr[:,iscell])\n450\t        neuropil.append(neuropil_curr[:,iscell])\n451\t    T = fluorescence[0].shape[0]\n452\t    # The rate stored on the series is the scanner rate: with two planes interleaved it is twice\n453\t    # the rate at which any one plane is sampled, which is why dff takes n_planes as well.\n454\t    frame_rate = plane_data.rate\n455\t    neural_time_bin_size = nplanes/frame_rate*1000 # ms\n456\t\n457\t    behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n458\t    \n459\t    timestamps = behavior_data['trial number'].timestamps[:] \n460\t    if T != len(timestamps):\n461\t        print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n462\t        T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n463\t        fluorescence = [f[:T] for f in fluorescence]\n464\t        neuropil = [f[:T] for f in neuropil]\n465\t        timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n466\t    \n467\t    behavior_time_bin_size = np.median(np.diff(timestamps))*1000 # ms\n468\t    \n469\t    # trial number for each time point\n470\t    trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], behavior_data['teleport'].data[:])\n471\t    ntrials = len(trial_start_idx)\n472\t    \n473\t    # all_info['reward_zone_positions'] = {}\n705\t    fig,ax = plt.subplots(1,len(subjects), figsize=(2*len(subjects),20),sharex=True,sharey=True)\n706\t\n707\t    for i,subject in                elif key not in all_info:\n730\t                    all_info[key] = value\n731\t                elif 'range' in key:\n732\t                    all_info[key] = (min(all_info[key][0],
